It's just a really heavy feeling most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling fatigued or like you don’t have energy most days, even when you haven’t been doing much physically or mentally taxing?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My energy levels are pretty normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the flip side, feeling restless or fidgety more often than not?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually. My coworkers have pointed it out. Sometimes I get really restless... like, I'll just pace around the restaurant, checking the same tables and looking over the same receipts over and over again. But then other times, it's the exact opposite. I get so slowed down that just grabbing menus and walking over to the POS system takes me way longer than it normally should. So, yeah, it kind of goes back and forth.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually guilty about things, or blaming yourself for things that aren’t really your fault?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. Actually, that's a big part of why I came in to see someone. I just keep feeling like I’ve completely let my family down by taking on this mortgage. It weighs on me a lot. And then at work, if I make even a small mistake, it just... it feels like it proves that I’m useless. It makes me feel like I deserve whatever bad things happen to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days where you don't take pleasure in things you normally enjoy, or where you just feel numb, even when you're not thinking about your worries?\n\nPatient: Yeah, most days are like that, actually. I'll be at work carrying plates, smiling at customers, doing all the things I'm supposed to do, but I'm not feeling anything good. It's just numb.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently—like eating much less or more than usual, or losing or gaining a noticeable amount of weight?\n\nPatient: Yeah, my eating has been pretty all over the place lately. But then other nights, I'll get home from a shift and just eat whatever's in the kitchen, mostly just to feel settled, I guess. So it really goes back and forth.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way — this heavy, empty feeling and the guilt about the mortgage and work mistakes?\n\nPatient: Well, the whole thing... this heavy feeling, the guilt, all of it... it's been going on for a while. But then other times, it's the exact opposite. I get so slowed down that just grabbing menus and walking over to the POS system takes me way longer than it normally should. So, yeah, it kind of goes back and forth.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually guilty about things, or blaming yourself for things that aren’t really your fault?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. Actually, that's a big part of why I came in to see someone. I just keep feeling like I’ve completely let my family down by taking on this mortgage. It weighs on me a lot. And then at work, if I make even a small mistake, it just... it feels like it proves that I’m useless. It makes me feel like I deserve whatever bad things happen to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days where you don't take pleasure in things you normally enjoy, or where you just feel numb, even when you're not thinking about your worries?\n\nPatient: Yeah, most days are like that, actually. There's just this... this heavy, empty feeling right in my chest. It's like I'm just going through the motions. I'll be at work carrying plates, smiling at customers, doing all the things I'm supposed to do, but I'm not feeling anything good. It's just numb.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently—like eating much less or more than usual, or losing or gaining a noticeable amount of weight?\n\nPatient: Yeah, my eating has been pretty all over the place lately. Some weeks I... I barely even touch the staff meal at work. I just don't have any appetite for it. But then other nights, I'll get home from a shift and just eat whatever's in the kitchen, mostly just to feel settled, I guess. I didn't have these long stretches of feeling this empty.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced periods where you felt unusually high, extremely energetic, or irritable, where you were doing or saying a lot more than usual, or where you needed much less sleep but still had lots of energy?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really had anything like that. It's just a normal amount of sleep, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period that started about a year ago, have you ever had an episode where you felt persistently down or lost interest in things for a period of at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: No, like I was saying earlier...
